1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In
curvature formulas, Tr(XY ) denotes this normalized invariant bilinear pairing; raw representation traces
are written Tr ρ(·). Let                     
                                              dG                    dG
                                      sG :=       + 1,      2sG >       .
                                               4                      2
The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
but its constants may depend on CG .

Proof. The normalized character satisfies Re Tr ρ(U ) ≤ dρ , with equality only when ρ(U ) = I; faithfulness
gives U = e. The Hessian statement follows from the chosen normalization and compactness. Up to a field-
independent constant, each plaquette factor is exp{t(χρ +χρ )} with t > 0. Its power series is a nonnegative
linear combination of characters of tensor products of ρ and ρ̄, and their irreducible decompositions
have nonnegative integer multiplicities. The standard character-expansion reflection-positivity argument
therefore applies.

1.3     Proof architecture
The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
   Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
                                                                                       Q
the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
   Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
   Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.

Only the theorem-level inputs in Table 1 are imported. The fixed-group geometry, action charge, higher
Sobolev landing, periodic-toron template theorem, invariant-jet admission, matching constants, and
noncollapse source are internal.

1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and
orientation covariance. The signed-corner path family is invariant under the lattice symmetry group.
At the flat point, the exact Fourier symbol is the color-free spacetime symbol tensored with Ig and has
acoustic value 2I ⊗ Ig and a uniformly gapped normal frame. The full-alias section and normal-fiber
contraction are proved in the later normal-chart chapters.
Proposition 2.3 (Exact coarea–fiber identity). At finite volume, in the normal fiber chart,

                                    dU = J0 det(I + DN P )−1 dW dζ,

where the determinant is the ordinary finite-dimensional determinant. The fixed linear determinant is
owned once as a scalar normalization, and the field-dependent logarithm is one connected analytic activity.

of the flat set.

4.3.3 Exact Gaussian disintegration and harmonic extension
Let C≥j be the mean-zero Feynman covariance before the scale-j block and let Bj = 12 Af,j be the
normalized linearized retained map. On the mean-zero coarse space define

                                 Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,        Jj = C≥j Bj∗ (Cjc )−1 ,

and
                                              Cjd = C≥j − Jj Cjc Jj∗ .
All inverses are taken only on the stated mean-zero spaces; torons remain in the retained compact
background.
Theorem 4.11 (Exact linear Gaussian fiber decomposition). The operators above satisfy

                           Bj Jj = I,        Bj Cjd = 0,       C≥j = Cjd + Jj Cjc Jj∗ .

If W ∼ N (0, Cjc ) and ζ ∼ N (0, Cjd ) are independent, then

                                                  A = Jj W + ζ

has covariance C≥j and Bj A = W . Conversely, this is the exact conditional disintegration of the fine
Feynman Gaussian given the retained linear block. In the folded Fourier frame, Cjd is the pushforward of
Copt from Corollary 4.10.
Proof. These are the finite-dimensional Gaussian Schur-complement identities. Since Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,
Bj Jj = I. Substitution gives Bj Cjd = 0 and the covariance sum. The characteristic function of Jj W + ζ is
therefore the one with covariance C≥j . The folded optical parameterization T (k) identifies the conditional
detail space with ker Af (k), and the conditional precision is the restriction of Hf to that space.

4.3.4 Exact Wilson–Haar factorization in the nonlinear fiber chart
Let PN (W, ζ) be the exact nonlinear normal-fiber chart proved in Sections 2.4– 3.2, and let Jcoarea be its
exact finite-volume coarea density. On the inner regular chart, write dγj (ζ) for the normalized Gaussian
with covariance Cjd .
Theorem 4.13 (Exact regular-reference identity). At finite volume and before any thermodynamic or
cutoff limit, the inner regular part of one Wilson fiber integral has the exact form
                Z                          Z              Z
                            −1
                   F (U )e−u SW (U ) dU = dW ρ0,j (W ) dγj (ζ) F (PN (W, ζ))e−Vj (W,ζ;u) .

sum of edge distances at least the root separation. The remaining branches are summed by the unused
rooted smallness margin. Fixed marks promote finitely many vertices and change only the constant.
4.4      Wick-stable jet block
Coordinate choice. Use the grounded covariances and harmonic extensions constructed in Section 4.3.
Thus, before the scale-j conditional expectation,
                                                    C≥j = Cj + Jj Cjc Jj∗ ,

where Cj = Cjd is the exact detail covariance and the second term is the embedded remaining covariance.
After canonical scale-two identification, Cjc is denoted C≥j+1 . For every finite-degree local gauge-complete
jet P , define
                                                          1
                                                                 
                                        : P : C≥j = exp − ∆C≥j P.
                                                          2
The contraction exponential terminates at finite degree.
   Let Πext extract:
1. the vacuum coordinate;
2. the tangent-normalized reflection-even Yang–Mills coordinate;
3. every declared marked/contact jet of degree below five;
4. total differences, Bianchi descendants, and the reflection-odd scalar sector.
   Put
                                                       Πirr = I − Πext
on the jet truncation through degree eight. The stable marked fiber begins at degree five; its unmarked
scalar subfiber begins at degree six.
Lemma 4.15 (Marked Gaussian Wick martingale). For every finite-degree gauge-complete jet, with zero,
one, or two active background marks and every fixed finite family of passive source leaves,

                                  ECj : P (Jj W + ζ) : C≥j =: (P ◦ Jj )(W ) : Cjc .

After the canonical scale-two identification this is the stated passage from scale j to scale j +1. Conditional
Gaussian expectation therefore preserves homogeneous Wick chaos exactly and creates no lower-chaos
contraction path.
Proof. This is Corollary 4.12. Explicitly,
                                                    C≥j = Cj + Jj Cjc Jj∗ ,
so the commuting contraction operators give
                             1          − 12 ∆C≥j                     − 21 ∆C c
                           e 2 ∆C j e               P (Jj W + ·) 0 = e        j   (P ◦ Jj )(W ).
The covariance is fixed rather than background dependent. Background and passive-source derivatives
therefore commute with the contraction operators. Wick contraction is applied to the complete gauge-
invariant tensor, so gauge, signed-hypercubic, and aligned-reflection covariance are preserved.

5.2     Standard-scheme conjugacy and coefficient
The coefficient is extracted from a local background renormalization condition on the full weak measure.
The background is not inserted as a freely translatable factor in an unrestricted Haar integral, and no
global gauge section or equality of globally gauge-fixed partition functions is asserted.

Proof. Apply Lemma 6.7 at the directed crossing depth. Equation (HT2) places the product of all empty-

                                 n =u

where the fixed optical template and both tail families have uniformly summable exponential rows with

   The regular factors in (HT1) are exactly the species of Proposition 4.26. The invariant-graph cone and

holomorphic or C ∞ through every order used here. Repeating the differentiated finite-dimensional score
and total-variation estimates for 0 ≤ h ≤ 2sG changes only the fixed constants. The permanent support
reporting is unchanged because these are internal pivot derivatives, not additional physical-source marks.
Reporting completeness in Lemma 6.7 proves that every atlas, partition, Jacobian, gauge-coordinate, and
inherited factor occurs in at least one row; canonical ownership proves it occurs in at most one. Thus the
three rows are exhaustive and disjoint, and all constants are independent of the number of weak steps.

6.3       Two-scale weak-to-Haar landing
Let PYM be the normalized marginal activity and let R⋆ (u) be the invariant irrelevant graph. Fix

                                             I⋆ = [ū, ū + Ccr ū2 ] ⋐ (0, u⋆ )

and 0 < a0 < a. The weak output radius is

                       Mwk := sup u∥PYM ∥a;3,N + ∥R⋆ (u)∥⋆;a;3,N + Ku3 .

This is a bound on the interaction relative to the scale-normalized Gaussian reference. It is not a
Haar-relative oscillation bound.
  Let F⋆ be the exact first-crossing family

                      Φ(u, ϱ) = uPYM + R⋆ (u) + ϱ,                     u ∈ I⋆ ,            ∥ϱ∥⋆;a;3,N ≤ Ku3 ,

Theorem 6.6 (Compact weak first-crossing class). The class C⋆ is compact in Ba0 ,2,N and every member
satisfies the weak-reference row bounded by Mwk , uniformly in finite volume, cutoff, exterior configuration,
and preceding weak depth. No Haar-relative smallness is asserted by this theorem.
Proof. The family is bounded in the stronger Ba,3,N topology. The support reserve e−(a−a0 )|X| and
Arzelà–Ascoli on the finitely many bounded rooted shapes give compactness from C 3 to C 2 .
Lemma 6.7 (Reporting completeness and depth telescoping). Let µj be the exact retained measure after
j weak steps, before the next logarithm is taken. For every finite depth n there is an exact representation
                                                   reg
                             dµj = Zj−1 exp{−Qref
                                              j − Rj } dHj + Ij ,                                  0 ≤ j ≤ n,   (HT1)

where dHj is product Haar on the retained links, Qref  j is the complete allocated scalar/two-jet reference,
Rjreg is the collected regular residual, and Ij is the recombined irregular operator. Every pre-logarithmic
factor of the exact step occurs in exactly one of these three rows, and the scalar normalizations satisfy
                                                                                    n−1
                                                                                    Y
                                        Zj+1 = Zj zj ,                Zn = Z0              zj ,                 (HT2)
                                                                                    j=0

with no zj reappearing as a nonempty-support activity. The representation and its zero/one/two marked
versions are uniform in depth.
Proof. The assertion is proved by induction on j. At j = 0, the Wilson–Haar measure and the exact
inner/collar/outer partition give (HT1): the complete quadratic reference is Qref
                                                                              0 , the post-two-jet inner
density is the regular row, and the recombined collar/outer/curvature labels are I0 .
   Assume (HT1) at step j. Apply the exact joint Gaussian/Karcher disintegration before taking a
connected logarithm. DAS3 retains the scalar normalization of that one joint law once at the exterior
endpoint; call it zj . The empty-support part therefore multiplies Zj and gives (HT2). All nonempty
factors are first reported on their actual local support or on the canonical hull of their exponential


                                                                54
connector. The factor inventory is exhaustive: Wilson words, product-Haar coordinate factors, coarea
and normal returns, gauge-coordinate endpoints, Gaussian/Schur endpoints, inherited activities, cutoffs,
and source leaves are exactly the ownership inventory in row (U1) of Theorem 5.27, implemented by
the one-step maps (E1)–(E6), together with the labelled inner/collar/outer partition. The labels sum
pointwise to one before logarithms, so an atlas-boundary factor cannot be lost between branches.
   Definition 3.22 assigns every reported nonempty factor to precisely one of the regular, one-core, or
cross rows; Lemma 3.25 does the same for every connected determinant word. Exact collect then replaces
each history by one canonical hull. The history-compatibility theorem computes the next allocation only
from this collected output, so neither a factor nor its scalar normalization is copied at a later depth. This
gives (HT1) at j + 1. The normalized structural/source marks commute with the finite induction and are
bounded by the same permanent rows, proving depth uniformity.

where the fixed optical template and both tail families have uniformly summable exponential rows with

removed all empty-support normalizations, and DAS2 uses the same scale-normalized optical template at
every step. Restoring product Haar measure converts the physical link logarithm only at the final scale.
The optical precision and its first two marked derivatives lie in the exponential matrix algebra. Summing
the finite-template incidences gives (42).
   The regular factors in (HT1) are exactly the species of Proposition 4.26. The invariant-graph cone and
output recertification therefore give (43). The recombined irregular row is bounded by Theorem 3.31,
giving (44). For the fixed group G, the Karcher, Haar, coarea, splice, and coordinate templates are
holomorphic or C ∞ through every order used here. Repeating the differentiated finite-dimensional score
and total-variation estimates for 0 ≤ h ≤ 2sG changes only the fixed constants. The permanent support
reporting is unchanged because these are internal pivot derivatives, not additional physical-source marks.
Reporting completeness in Lemma 6.7 proves that every atlas, partition, Jacobian, gauge-coordinate, and
inherited factor occurs in at least one row; canonical ownership proves it occurs in at most one. Thus the
three rows are exhaustive and disjoint, and all constants are independent of the number of weak steps.

distinguished insertions and are never absorbed into a one-pivot density or counted as physical activities.

every step. Restoring product Haar measure converts the physical link logarithm only at the final scale.
The optical precision and its first two marked derivatives lie in the exponential matrix algebra. Summing
the finite-template incidences gives (42).
   The regular factors in (HT1) are exactly the species of Proposition 4.26. The invariant-graph cone and
output recertification therefore give (43). The recombined irregular row is bounded by Theorem 3.31,
giving (44). For the fixed group G, the Karcher, Haar, coarea, splice, and coordinate templates are
holomorphic or C ∞ through every order used here. Repeating the differentiated finite-dimensional score
and total-variation estimates for 0 ≤ h ≤ 2sG changes only the fixed constants. The permanent support
reporting is unchanged because these are internal pivot derivatives, not additional physical-source marks.
Reporting completeness in Lemma 6.7 proves that every atlas, partition, Jacobian, gauge-coordinate, and
inherited factor occurs in at least one row; canonical ownership proves it occurs in at most one. Thus the
three rows are exhaustive and disjoint, and all constants are independent of the number of weak steps.

6.5.3 Final compact Haar-pivot collect
In (53), write U for the compact matching links and keep the complete optical field ζ and the actual
local source roots unchanged. Apply the exact compact-only path-product block BL to U as a finite
matching operation. Corollary 6.19 permits coefficientwise use of its hybrid chart, while every compact
path detail is integrated in the same complete joint partition function. No optical variable is identified
with a compact path detail, and no source is assumed fiber-constant under BL . The block is used once to
place many same-law Haar pivots inside each coarse compact edge.
Definition 6.21 (Matching-stage tri-graded norm). For a compact-stage activity family F = (FX ) and
fixed positive mark weights σpiv , σζ , σJ , set
                                                                  |α|
                                               X                 σpiv σζr σJq
                       ∥F ∥a;2sG |3|2 := sup         ea|X| max                 α

                                               X∋p       0≤r≤3
                                                         0≤q≤2

Here Dpiv differentiates only the declared live original-link pivot variables. The derivative Dζ is a Fréchet
derivative in the unintegrated optical coordinate at the compact-collect call site, and DJ differentiates
the physical source. The three indices are independent; no optical derivative is counted as a structural
pivot derivative or as a source leaf. The pivot order 2sG is the fixed-group Sobolev order required by the
product Peter–Weyl landing; the optical order three and the physical-source order two are unchanged
from the permanent marked hierarchy.
Lemma 6.22 (Joint regular pivot–optical–source tube). There are positive radii ρpiv , ρζ , ρJ , independent
of cutoff, volume, exterior configuration, and weak depth, such that every reference, regular, coarea,
determinant, and Gaussian-normalization factor QX has a jointly holomorphic extension to the product

of (i) the complexified live-pivot chart polydisc of radius ρpiv and (ii) the common optical/source species

                                                            |α|

                                                   0≤q≤2

Thus Definition 6.21 applies to this sector with any fixed σpiv ≤ ρpiv , σζ ≤ ρζ , and σJ ≤ ρJ .
Proof. The finite regular chart atlas gives a common complex live-pivot polydisc, and Lemma 3.27(iii)
gives the common optical/source species tube. On each chart the exact reference, coarea, determinant,
Gaussian-normalization, and regular recombination formulas are finite compositions of local coefficient
maps analytic in all three displayed variables, together with multiplication, determinant, and inversion.
The real denominators already have a uniform positive lower bound, so one fixed shrinking of the three
radii keeps every complexified denominator away from zero. The same formulas therefore extend jointly
to the Cartesian product of the two printed domains. The atlas is finite, so the radii and the supremum
constant are uniform. Multivariable Cauchy on the product tube then gives (57). The rooted exponential
weight is unchanged because Cauchy acts only in the three local coefficient variables.
Lemma 6.23 (Tri-graded optical/source upgrade). In the exact factorization of Theorem 6.10, the
normalized one-pivot logarithmic scores, VA,J , zA,J = e−VA,J − 1, the exact positive denominators, and
the recombined regular/irregular/coarea/determinant factors obey one cutoff-, volume-, exterior-, and
weak-depth-uniform ∥ · ∥a;2sG |3|2 row. Every marked physical occurrence is owned exactly once. A mark
on a one-pivot reference removes at most one unmarked Haar payment; a mark on a multipivot factor is
paid by the corresponding tri-graded charged row.
Proof. Differentiate the exact compact factorization before integrating the optical field. At this call site ζ
is an exterior coefficient variable: the compact pivot integration, the Peter–Weyl cut, and the BKAR
interpolation parameters are held fixed. Consequently Dζ differentiates only the local coefficient atoms
and the finite one-core normalizations already present in the exact factorization; it does not create an
additional BKAR covariance derivative.
    For the reference, regular, coarea, determinant, and Gaussian-normalization factors, apply Lemma 6.22
to the entire regular sector. For an inner-chart irregular component, the absolute component row already
holds on the joint exterior/optical/source product tube; multivariable Cauchy gives the mixed derivatives
with |α| ≤ 2sG , r ≤ 3, and q ≤ 2. Transition and outer labels are finite-dimensional smooth densities on
the same charged event and are differentiated directly. Their fixed Bell-polynomial loss is polynomial in
the component size and is absorbed by the bad-action exponential.
   The maps V 7→ e−V − 1, multiplication, inversion of the strictly positive scalar denominator, and
the normalized logarithmic-score map are analytic maps of this finite-order tri-graded Banach algebra.
Leibniz, Faà di Bruno, and the quotient rule are paid by the three factorial weights in (56). Since G
is fixed, the derivative orders 2sG , three, and two are fixed finite integers, so the resulting constant is
uniform in all declared limits. This proves the stated tri-graded row without identifying an optical field
derivative with a passive source derivative.

Lemma 6.25 (Complete compact-flux classification and canonical exploration). Fix ζ and take the
Peter–Weyl decomposition of the complete compact incidence network generated by the exact pushforward
under BL . Integrate all compact variables coefficientwise. A connected history is compact-vacuum only
when every compact cut separating distinct mixed optical/source vertices carries the trivial representation.
After removal of already reported physical cross-core connectors, such a history factorizes into one-core
expectations or empty-support scalars and is assigned by the exact owner map to the normalized one-core
references or the pre-existing regular/Gaussian inventory.
   Every remaining history, including a multi-leg singlet with trivial visible coarse label but nontrivial

unoriented edge inspected. Discovery edges form a rooted spanning tree; each merger, reconnection, shared
tail, cycle, seam, reflection, or wrapping edge is a uniquely owned closure edge. The record reconstructs
the occupied geometric network without duplicating shared continuations.

Proof. Vacuum factorization follows by deleting the reported physical connectors and applying Fubini
across every trivial compact cut. Otherwise the least mixed cut, least nontrivial representation label, and
least oriented half-edge choose the root and first edge. Deterministic depth-first exploration then classifies
every occupied unoriented edge exactly once as a discovery or closure edge. A composite singlet is retained
whenever any internal separating cut is nontrivial, independently of the visible output label.

6.5.3 Final compact Haar-pivot collect
In (53), write U for the compact matching links and keep the complete optical field ζ and the actual
local source roots unchanged. Apply the exact compact-only path-product block BL to U as a finite
matching operation. Corollary 6.19 permits coefficientwise use of its hybrid chart, while every compact
path detail is integrated in the same complete joint partition function. No optical variable is identified
with a compact path detail, and no source is assumed fiber-constant under BL . The block is used once to
place many same-law Haar pivots inside each coarse compact edge.

the physical source. The three indices are independent; no optical derivative is counted as a structural
pivot derivative or as a source leaf. The pivot order 2sG is the fixed-group Sobolev order required by the
product Peter–Weyl landing; the optical order three and the physical-source order two are unchanged
from the permanent marked hierarchy.

Proof. The finite regular chart atlas gives a common complex live-pivot polydisc, and Lemma 3.27(iii)

Lemma 6.23 (Tri-graded optical/source upgrade). In the exact factorization of Theorem 6.10, the
normalized one-pivot logarithmic scores, VA,J , zA,J = e−VA,J − 1, the exact positive denominators, and
the recombined regular/irregular/coarea/determinant factors obey one cutoff-, volume-, exterior-, and

paid by the corresponding tri-graded charged row.
Proof. Differentiate the exact compact factorization before integrating the optical field. At this call site ζ
is an exterior coefficient variable: the compact pivot integration, the Peter–Weyl cut, and the BKAR
interpolation parameters are held fixed. Consequently Dζ differentiates only the local coefficient atoms
and the finite one-core normalizations already present in the exact factorization; it does not create an
additional BKAR covariance derivative.
    For the reference, regular, coarea, determinant, and Gaussian-normalization factors, apply Lemma 6.22
to the entire regular sector. For an inner-chart irregular component, the absolute component row already
holds on the joint exterior/optical/source product tube; multivariable Cauchy gives the mixed derivatives
with |α| ≤ 2sG , r ≤ 3, and q ≤ 2. Transition and outer labels are finite-dimensional smooth densities on
the same charged event and are differentiated directly. Their fixed Bell-polynomial loss is polynomial in
the component size and is absorbed by the bad-action exponential.
   The maps V 7→ e−V − 1, multiplication, inversion of the strictly positive scalar denominator, and
the normalized logarithmic-score map are analytic maps of this finite-order tri-graded Banach algebra.
Leibniz, Faà di Bruno, and the quotient rule are paid by the three factorial weights in (56). Since G
is fixed, the derivative orders 2sG , three, and two are fixed finite integers, so the resulting constant is
uniform in all declared limits. This proves the stated tri-graded row without identifying an optical field
derivative with a passive source derivative.

Proof. Triviality on every separating compact cut permits repeated Haar–Fubini separation. The exact

Theorem 7.7 (Exact acoustic exclusion-or-capture). Consider any connected zero-, one-, or two-source
coefficient joining two bounded matching-scale root buffers in the exact joint logarithm. At least one object
on the unique root-to-root forest path belongs to one of the following classes:
   (i) a nonvacuum compact activity controlled by Theorem 6.39 and the spatial row (99);
  (ii) an optical covariance bond or an optical Gaussian cross-core activity in AJG ;
 (iii) a registered reference, regular, irregular, seam, reflection, wrapping, normalization, determinant, or
       source connector with its original support payment.
There is no fourth class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge.
Proof. Assume that a connected coefficient contains no object of types (i)–(iii). Absence of (i) makes
every separating compact cut vacuum. Absence of (iii) leaves no registered physical connector after the
complete owner map. Lemma 7.6 then reduces the compact contribution to disconnected one-core factors
and scalars. Absence of (ii) also prevents the still-live optical field from joining the roots. The coefficient
is therefore disconnected, a contradiction. The only possible remaining concern would be an undeclared
massless retained covariance, which is excluded by Lemma 7.5.

Corollary 7.8 (No additional acoustic path class). The exact vertex inventory used in Theorem 6.43 ad-
mits no fourth root-to-root path class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge. Consequently
the boundary-to-center estimates (104) and (107) require no additional terminal Witten, acoustic-channel,
or retained-Gaussian gap hypothesis.
Proof. The vertex inventory is already exhaustive by Lemma 6.7, Proposition 6.8, and Corollary 6.40.
Apply Theorem 7.7 to exclude the only remaining acoustic fourth-class concern.

Connectors at one scale The exact iterated conditional logarithm is divided into two propagation
regimes.
(R1) There is a fixed weak threshold jw such that every finer scale j ≥ jw lies in one strict rooted expansion
     ball. After distributing the support weight in the tree estimate, every additional nonreference vertex
     pays a common branch factor σ < 1. The exact Karcher detail covariance obeys the exponential
     scale-local bond of Corollary 4.14; fixed connector words have physical diameter O(ℓj ), and the
     weak/irregular recombination has a summable rooted remainder.
(R2) The finitely many scales between jw and the compact matching scale are integrated inside the finite
     macrobridge. Their source dependence is absorbed into bounded matching-scale local atoms; they
     are not counted as independent long-distance connectors.
(R3) At the matching scale, Theorem 6.43 gives the exact joint Haar–optical two-buffer connector, and
     Theorem 6.44 permits each buffer to contain an arbitrary fixed finite local source cluster, with
     common spacetime exponent mE > 0. Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional unreported massless
     acoustic reference channel beyond the exact owner-map inventory.
(R4) One or two bounded or normalized source buffers may remain as declared roots with row and
     column bounds uniform in all exterior variables.

Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write

                                     Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
   A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.

derivative order n,                                        X

                                                      τ ∈Tm,n

where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115

Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write

                                     Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
   A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,                                        X
                             Dn Wm (0)[J1 , . . . , Jn ] =   Am (τ ; J1 , . . . , Jn ),                 (115)
                                                      τ ∈Tm,n

where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.

    For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.
Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2 Wm (0) is
the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw .
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.

Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional
logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.
Lemma 8.8 (Canonical multiscale allocation). Every connected two-buffer source history is assigned
exactly once either to the matching connector or to one weak scale j ≥ jw . Finer decorations attached to
its coarsest skeleton have a uniformly summable one/two-root norm. Put

                                             dw := CR + m−1
                                                         w .



                                                     75
For d ≥ dw ,                   X
                                      Ce−c0 | log σ|(d−CR )/ℓj ≤ C ′ e−mw (d−CR )/2 .                  (116)
                               j≥jw

For d < dw the complete history is retained in one fixed enlarged local cluster: it is uniformly bounded for
bounded observables and controlled in the local source topology for renormalized fields.
Proof. By Corollary 8.7, the lowest common ancestor of the two source buffers assigns every connected
tree to exactly one terminal or weak-scale class. This is the first scale in the exact recursive conditional-
logarithm formula at which the two source components are joined. The least-label convention removes
automorphism multiplicities before estimates are applied. The fixed root algebra sums every finer
decoration independently of the number of activePnonroot supports. Since ℓj = 2−(j−jw ) ℓjw , put
x = mw (d − CR )+ . The scale sum is bounded by C r≥0 exp(−2r x). The condition d ≥ dw gives x ≥ 1,
and then this is at most C1 exp(−x), proving (116). If d < dw the two buffers lie in one fixed enlarged
physical neighborhood. Their ultraviolet contact subtrees are not summed as separate long connectors;
they are contracted into the exact local atom described in the statement.
Theorem 8.9 (Uniform physical two-source connector). There are dloc , A0 , m > 0, independent of
resolution, microscopic cutoff, volume, and boundary conditions, with the following properties.
 (a) For two centered bounded positive-scale observables supported in BR (z) and BR (z ′ ), the complete
     connected majorant is bounded at every separation by
                                                                                       ′
                            Cm (BR (z), BR (z ′ )) ≤ Km (z, z ′ ) := A0 e−m(|z−z |1 −2R)+ ,            (117)

     and                                 X                             X
                               sup ℓ4m        Km (z, z ′ ) + sup ℓ4m       Km (z, z ′ ) ≤ K0 .         (118)
                                z                              z′
                                         z′                            z

 (b) If either buffer carries a fixed finite renormalized local source, displacement mark, or rotation mark,
     then for test sources with disjoint supports at distance d ≥ dloc the connected functional is bounded
     by Cs,A,B e−md . At shorter distance it is controlled in the local Schwartz/source seminorm supplied
     by the triangular finite-jet renormalization; no cutoff-uniform pointwise estimate on the diagonal is
     claimed.
The exponent m is common to both classes and to every fixed finite source family.
Proof. Combine Lemma 8.5 with the exact forest allocation and the weak scale sum. For d ≥ dloc choose
dloc so the variable x = mw (d − CR )+ in Lemma 8.8 is at least one. Weak histories then decay with
rate at least mw /2, while matching histories decay with rate at least 12 min{mtr , msp }. Hence one may
take the common exponent m = m∗ from (111); Lemma 8.2 shows explicitly that this exponent is not
degraded by increasing the ultraviolet depth.
   For bounded observables, the covariance and every short-distance connected local atom are bounded
by their sup norms, so enlarging A0 extends the bound to all separations. Lemma 8.1 and integrability of
the physical exponential kernel give (118). For a normalized local source, the short-distance cluster is
not estimated pointwise. It is exactly the local source coefficient renormalized by the finite triangular
counterterm matrix; the finite triangular source theorem printed in Part V bounds it in a finite Schwartz
seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both
local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent
physical rate without suppressing or hiding contact terms.

                                                     74
Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write

                                     Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
   A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,                                        X
                             Dn Wm (0)[J1 , . . . , Jn ] =   Am (τ ; J1 , . . . , Jn ),                 (115)
                                                      τ ∈Tm,n

where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.

    For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.
Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2 Wm (0) is
the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw .
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.

Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional
logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.
Lemma 8.8 (Canonical multiscale allocation). Every connected two-buffer source history is assigned
exactly once either to the matching connector or to one weak scale j ≥ jw . Finer decorations attached to
its coarsest skeleton have a uniformly summable one/two-root norm. Put

                                             dw := CR + m−1
                                                         w .



                                                     75
For d ≥ dw ,                   X
                                      Ce−c0 | log σ|(d−CR )/ℓj ≤ C ′ e−mw (d−CR )/2 .                  (116)
                               j≥jw

For d < dw the complete history is retained in one fixed enlarged local cluster: it is uniformly bounded for
bounded observables and controlled in the local source topology for renormalized fields.
Proof. By Corollary 8.7, the lowest common ancestor of the two source buffers assigns every connected
tree to exactly one terminal or weak-scale class. This is the first scale in the exact recursive conditional-
logarithm formula at which the two source components are joined. The least-label convention removes
automorphism multiplicities before estimates are applied. The fixed root algebra sums every finer
decoration independently of the number of activePnonroot supports. Since ℓj = 2−(j−jw ) ℓjw , put
x = mw (d − CR )+ . The scale sum is bounded by C r≥0 exp(−2r x). The condition d ≥ dw gives x ≥ 1,
and then this is at most C1 exp(−x), proving (116). If d < dw the two buffers lie in one fixed enlarged
physical neighborhood. Their ultraviolet contact subtrees are not summed as separate long connectors;
they are contracted into the exact local atom described in the statement.

 (a) For two centered bounded positive-scale observables supported in BR (z) and BR (z ′ ), the complete

Lemma 8.14 (Reflection positivity inheritance under exact recombination). Every limiting positive-time
quadratic form used in the common Schwinger system is the limit of a reflection-positive quadratic form
under the original Wilson measure. Exact normal-chart, coarea, compact-pivot, and source transfor-

regular/irregular and half-cylinder labels are recombined before the OS form is taken. Therefore reflection

                                           X

The equality is unchanged by the exact coordinate transformations because all Jacobian and normalization
factors remain inside the same normalized integral once. Every entry of the finite matrix converges along
the common subsequence, so the closed positive-semidefinite cone contains the limiting matrix. This
proves the claim.
Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,
permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for
the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized
local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential
clustering.
Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over
a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes
as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact
label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12
gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row.

8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
algebra g:
(I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
(I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
(I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
     even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
(I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
     tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
      presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
occur.

Lemma 8.14 (Reflection positivity inheritance under exact recombination). Every limiting positive-time
quadratic form used in the common Schwinger system is the limit of a reflection-positive quadratic form
under the original Wilson measure. Exact normal-chart, coarea, compact-pivot, and source transfor-
mations are changes of variables or observable identities, not replacement probability measures, and all
regular/irregular and half-cylinder labels are recombined before the OS form is taken. Therefore reflection
positivity passes to the common limiting family.
Proof. At finite cutoff and aligned reflection volume, let Fi be any finite family of constructed positive-time
observables. Pull each Fi back through the exact block, fiber, and source identities to a gauge-invariant
function of the original positive-time links. Exact recombination makes this pullback independent of the
auxiliary labels. Reflection positivity of the Wilson measure gives
                                           X
                                               ci cj ⟨ΘFi Fj ⟩a,L ≥ 0.
                                           i,j

The equality is unchanged by the exact coordinate transformations because all Jacobian and normalization
factors remain inside the same normalized integral once. Every entry of the finite matrix converges along
the common subsequence, so the closed positive-semidefinite cone contains the limiting matrix. This
proves the claim.
Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,
permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for
the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized
local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential
clustering.
Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over
a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes
as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact
label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12
gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row.

8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
algebra g:
(I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
(I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
(I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
     even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
(I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
     tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
      presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
occur.

Lemma 8.17 (Exact finite-lattice structure). On the full compact link manifold, Xa,v      cl
                                                                                             is globally smooth,
linear in v, finite range, gauge equivariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and reflection compatible. All
group derivatives of its local coefficient through arbitrary fixed order are bounded independently of volume,
apart from the explicit canonical factor a−1 in (124).
Proof. The projected sine coordinate (122) is a smooth matrix function followed by the fixed equivariant
projection Πρ , hence is smooth on the whole group. Under a lattice gauge transformation, a based
plaquette is conjugated by the gauge value at its base point; conjugation equivariance of Ωsin gives
Cµν (x) 7→ g(x)Cµν (x)g(x)−1 . Consequently Yx,ν Uν (x) transforms as a tangent vector to the link from x
to x + aν̂. Signed permutations reindex the two oriented plaquettes and the profile components, while
the orientation sign in (123) is exactly the sign of the transformed two-form. All derivative bounds follow
from finite support and compactness of the group.

Lemma 8.18 (The vacuum diagonal block vanishes edgewise). At U = 1,
                                       DAν (x) Cµν (x) U =1 = 0     (µ ̸= ν),                             (126)

                                                   Trg ad(q) = 0.                                         (128)
Thus the Haar divergence has neither a vacuum term nor a field-linear gauge-invariant term.

for one finite Schwartz seminorm pN depending on the source types. If the source labels split into two
groups with mutually separated supports, the connected cumulant has the additional common factor e−md .
Proof. Differentiate the connected source logarithm and first collect each maximal contact subtree into
one local root. Apply Lemma 8.10; Theorem 6.44 shows that no matching-stage derivative of order
n is required, because each tree edge sees only its two endpoint clusters as distinguished roots. For
bounded roots, every edge has the pointwise connector (117); excess attachments are summed by the
unused strict KP margin, and the deterministic allocation costs at most the displayed factorial. For
renormalized local sources, first contract every contact subtree into its finite local source vertex. The
remaining edges join disjoint clusters and carry the separated-support exponential from Theorem 8.9(b).
The local vertices are bounded by the triangular source-renormalization seminorms. Repeated Schur
summation of the remaining edges, with one Schwartz L1 norm at the tree root, gives (120) and the
separated-group exponential.
Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

11     Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups
Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple
G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
with Ig , so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
with the universal color factor CA (G).
   The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG , and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG , the first proof
call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate
uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is
color-free, tensors with Ig , or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space.
Corollary 11.2 (The SU(N ) family). For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N )
in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is
                                               11N log 2
                             u(2L) = u(L) +              u(L)2 + ON (u(L)3 ).
                                                 24π 2
No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted.
Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. The directed subsequence,
curvature-jet observable scope, and absence of a large-rank uniformity claim are part of the statement,
not omitted conclusions.

observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet.
   The scale factor in (189) is instead purely geometric. In the tangent-normalized profile coordinates, one
lattice difference at representation scale ℓi = 2i r is (r/ℓi ) times the corresponding unit-scale difference.
The two explicit factors in (188) therefore contribute exactly (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i . The two support moments
of Sλ are absorbed by the unused rooted exponent. This argument applies to the whole packet, local and
nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C 2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives
do not remove the two explicit differences.


                                                       95
Lemma A.4 (g-marked one-scale admission). There is g0 > 0 such that, for real 0 ≤ g ≤ g0 , the complete
normalized interaction and all declared source/defect marks are twice continuously differentiable in the
permanent regulator space. An undistinguished non-Gaussian child has parent-normalized row

                                                 η0 (g) ≤ Cg,

while a child carrying one or two distinguished g derivatives has row

                                              η1 (g) + η2 (g) ≤ C.

The exactly recombined large-field/irregular row and its first two g derivatives extend continuously by zero
                                             2
at g = 0 and are bounded by Cm g −pm e−c/g for m ≤ 2. No claim that an attached odd child is O(g 2 ) is
made.
Proof. In the scale-normalized Gaussian coordinate, the cubic Yang–Mills species starts at order g, the
quartic and other non-Gaussian even species start at order g 2 , and the common species tube controls
all field and source marks. Thus an unmarked species row is Cg, while one or two explicit g derivatives
remove at most the corresponding powers and give a finite row. The regulator allocations are additive
under multiplication, and the Gram and Pruefer estimates in the summed replica–BKAR theorem are
insensitive to the parity of an attached tensor. The ordinary parent-normalized child recursion therefore
closes for Cg0 below its printed threshold, with two fixed derivative marks handled by Leibniz. The
C 3 -flat irregular estimate in u = g 2 gives the displayed first and second g-derivative rows after the chain
rule. In particular the model child gA3 differentiated once by a covariance edge is correctly admitted as
an O(g) child, and one g derivative of that child is O(1).
Lemma A.5 (Cellwise complete shell decomposition). Fix the finite directed depth M and one crossing
cell, including the no-crossing cell. After the selected exact factorization has been completed, apply the
canonical reported-support selector only to whole normalized packets. The separated part of the two-root
functional has an exact disjoint decomposition
                                                       X
                                          Qb sep (g) =    FM,k (g)                                   (190)
                                             r,M
                                                       k≥0

whose coefficients are C 2 on the relative interior of that cell. Every normalization, denominator, localiza-
tion, irregular and source-motion contact belongs to exactly one selected packet. No assertion that the
individual coefficients FM,k agree for two different stopping factorizations is needed or made.
Proof. At fixed finite volume and depth, the reporting-telescope and owner-map identities form a disjoint
finite or absolutely convergent inventory of complete normalized packets. Their canonical physical reported
supports therefore admit disjoint scalar dyadic selectors after packet completion. Inside one crossing
cell the stopping depth and exact factorization are fixed, so the graph-segment C 2 identity and the
finite marked forest permit termwise differentiation. Summing the selectors gives the unselected exact
functional, proving (190).
Definition A.6 (Finite directed continuation and crossing cells). Fix the physical weak scale r, its
               √
endpoint gr = ur , and an arbitrary finite further directed depth M . Choose one terminal coarse side
Mout and set Mi = 2M −i Mout . The side is first chosen admissible for Lemma 6.1 and is enlarged once,
independently of M , to satisfy the fixed-torus smallness threshold of Theorem A.9. For every 0 ≤ g ≤ gr ,
use the exact weak recurrence

               u0 (g) = g 2 ,    ui+1 (g) = Fi (ui (g)),   gi (g) = ui (g),   0 ≤ i < M.
                                                                   p

Let
                                  NM (g) := min{0 ≤ i ≤ M : ui (g) ≥ ū},
with NM (g) = ∞ if the set is empty. Uniform outgoing transversality implies that, when NM (g) < ∞,

                               ui (g) < ū   (i < NM (g)),      uNM (g) (g) ∈ I⋆ .

The relative interiors of the sets on which NM (g) = N are called the crossing cells. On such a cell the
exact factorization consists of N weak conditionals followed by the printed finite macrobridge and joint
compact–optical logarithm. On the no-crossing cell it consists of all M weak conditionals followed by the
terminal weak logarithm on the fixed coarse torus.


                                                      96
   This is a choice of exact factorization, not a modification of the measure. The integer NM (g) is never
differentiated. Derivatives are taken inside one crossing cell, where the depth is fixed, and are extended
to cell boundaries using the original finite-volume C 2 normalized functional. At g = 0 one always uses
the no-crossing factorization and later sends M → ∞.
Definition A.7 (Canonical finite-volume off-graph coordinates). For the directed schedule in Defini-
tion A.6, put
                           εi := e−σMi ,     δi := δG (Mi ),    Mi = 2M −i Mout .
The first quantity records genuine wrapping histories in the interaction; the second records the scalar
massless finite-size comparison. After each complete connected output has been formed and assigned its
canonical reported hull, let Piwr select precisely the history certificates containing at least one periodic
wrapping occurrence at some scale not later than i. If Ri is the exact finite-volume centered interaction
and R⋆ (ui ) is the rooted-thermodynamic invariant graph, set

                      Ωi := Ri − R⋆ (ui ),       Wi := Piwr Ωi ,     Bi := (I − Piwr )Ωi .               (191)

Thus Ωi = Wi + Bi coefficientwise. Both Ri and R⋆ (ui ) are written in the same exact schedule-fixed
vacuum and tangent-normalized coupling coordinates. The coordinate intertwining and supportwise
                                    [2]
extraction identity therefore give EX Ωi,X = 0 for every reported support X; all three off-graph coordinates
lie in the actual centered stable fiber. The first coordinate is the canonical sum of histories carrying a
wrapping certificate. After the tangent-normalized scalar coordinate is extracted, the massless acoustic
sum–integral correction belongs to the nonautonomous scalar map Fi and is not an off-graph activity.
By the exact regular interaction comparison and label recombination, the only nonwrapping off-graph
difference is the slaved U4 charged/atlas complement; this is Bi and is exponentially flat in ui . No flat
charged coefficient is identified with a wrapping history.

Proof. Use the complete labelled connected-history representation before forgetting owners. In the
